Right-looking blocked algorithm:\n\nFor kblock from 0 to n step b:\n  - Factor diagonal block A[k:k+b, k:k+b] (unblocked, sequential or parallelized internally)\n  - Solve panel A[k+b:n, k:k+b] = L21 * L11^{-T} (TRSM) parallel over rows\n  - Update trailing A[k+b:n, k+b:n] -= L21 * L21^T (SYRK) parallel over tiles/rows\n\nNeed careful zeroing upper part. That gives parallel speedup for off-diagonal columns, scaling limited by n-j work per row. For n=512 and typical threads, this may be decent but not great for large core counts; also outer j loop has dependency barrier. Dot product length grows. This is easy and correct, but performance maybe ~ memory-bound. Blocked algorithm with GEMM-like updates has much better arithmetic intensity and cache reuse.\n\nWe want \"optimized for maximum performance and parallel scalability\". Implement blocked right-looking with OpenMP tasks or loops, with tiling for cache. Could also parallelize the update with register blocking.\n\nConsider using OpenMP task-based tiled Cholesky (like PLASMA/OpenMP). Recursive algorithm:\n\ncholesky_recursive(A, n, b, offset):\n if n <= b: unblocked factor\n else:\n   half = ... (multiple of b)\n   #pragma omp task\n   cholesky_recursive(A00, half)\n   #pragma omp taskwait\n   #pragma omp taskgroup\n   {\n     for each row block i in panel of half..n step b:\n       #pragma omp task\n       trsm(A[i,0:half], A00)  // solve block row\n     for each tile (i,j) in trailing:\n       #pragma omp task\n       gemm update\n   }\n   cholesky_recursive(A11, n-half)\n \nThis has good parallelism. Not with row-major original because each j depends on prior computed L[i,k] in same row. So unblocked outer-i parallelization is impossible.
